    **⇒ THE MEASURED BAR #5 IS VALID ONLY WHILE ≤ 8 KINDS ARE ALIVE.** The spike deliberately prints all **13** fixture kinds and does not honour the cap (a declared deviation, `SiegeLlamaSpike.cpp:318`, and the right call — honouring it would have converted six *model* results into six *harness* artefacts). **Shipped accuracy on a >8-kind board will therefore be WORSE than measured.** ⚠️ **This condition travels ATTACHED TO THE NUMBER** — wherever bar #5 is quoted (headline, checkpoint, handoff, Slack, `Docs/`) the condition is quoted with it. A measured result whose precondition gets dropped in the second re-telling is how a measurement becomes a false promise.
- **THREADING/LIFETIME:** `USiegeLlamaSubsystem : UGameInstanceSubsystem` (survives level travel, so Play Again never reloads 2.5 GB — the `USiegeSessionSubsystem` precedent) · **one dedicated `FRunnable` at `TPri_BelowNormal`** (`llama_context` is not thread-safe; a long-lived runnable makes single-thread ownership structural rather than a comment) · **queue depth 1, enforced by the FSM** (input disabled while thinking — this deletes the out-of-order/stale-context bug class entirely) · **cancellation via `llama_context_params::abort_callback`** — necessary, and the only thing that can interrupt a long prefill at all (polling after generation is far too late), but **NOT sufficient on GPU: see the footnote below** · three timeouts: **soft 4 s** (UX only, keeps waiting), **hard 10 s** (abort — a promise on CPU, a *request* on GPU), **token budget 96** · **the completion delegate fires on the GAME THREAD**, always.
  - **⚠️ `abort_callback` IS DOCUMENTED CPU-ONLY, SO THE 10 s DEADLINE IS NOT A GPU GUARANTEE — CORRECTED 2026-08-02 FROM THE VENDORED HEADER (`llama.h:382-385`, confirmed at the TASK-412 gate, `qa/TASK-412.md` §9).** The header says it in as many words: *"Abort callback / if it returns true, execution of `llama_decode()` will be aborted / **currently works only with CPU execution**"*. On the **CPU-only tier** it does bound a run mid-decode, which is what this section always assumed. On the **full-offload and partial tiers it cannot interrupt work already submitted to the device** — the hard deadline can only fire **between** graph submissions, so a submission in flight runs to completion whatever the callback returns. ⇒ **On GPU the token budget (`MaxOutputTokens` 96) is the primary bound on run length and the deadline is the coarse backstop — the reverse of how §10 reads.** `abort_callback` stays mandatory (still free, still the only prefill-length lever on CPU), but *"the only correct mechanism"* was **wrong as written: necessary, never sufficient.** ⚠️ **A later task that lowers or drops `MaxOutputTokens` because "the 10 s abort covers us" is deleting the GPU tiers' only real bound.** Whoever measures the GPU tiers reports the **worst observed delay between abort request and worker exit**, not the deadline value — an unmeasured deadline is an assumption, and this one is header-documented as false on two of the three tiers.
  - **⚠️ `use_mmap` NO LONGER EXISTS IN THE VENDORED BUILD — THE FIELD MOVED, THE REQUIREMENT DID NOT (b10235; `llama.h:306-322` + `:205-211`, confirmed at the TASK-412 gate, `qa/TASK-412.md` §9).** `struct llama_model_params` has **no `use_mmap` member**; mmap-backed loading is selected by **`ModelParams.load_mode = LLAMA_LOAD_MODE_MMAP`** (`enum llama_load_mode`, value 1), which is what the spike already sets. **Read this as a rename, NOT a relaxation:** mmap loading remains **load-bearing for the modal-8 GB-card claim** (§7) — the weights stay page-cache-backed rather than resident — so a build that leaves `load_mode` at the default has silently dropped the assumption bar #4 is measured against, and it will do so **without a compile error**, which is why it is recorded here rather than left to be rediscovered. ⚠️ **TASK-423 is specced against this section:** `use_mmap` is the name a search will fail to find, and **"the field is gone" must never be read as "the requirement is gone."**
- **A GGML CRASH MUST NOT KILL THE GAME.** SEH around the eval, a **`bAssistantFaulted` session latch**, and a model-load failure that **never blocks match start**.
- **MULTIPLAYER — V1 IS HOST/STANDALONE ONLY, FORCED, NOT CHOSEN.** Group state is not replicated, and `SetUnitCommand` / `BeginGroupPick` / `PlayHandSlot` / `DiscardHandSlot` all carry the D5 observer lockout. **The assistant must never be more capable than the keys:** it refuses on `!HasAuthority()` with the same approved wording. The console still opens and says so — silence would read as a bug.
  - **⛔ DO NOT WRITE A `ServerExecuteAssistantCommand` RPC IN V1.** It would be a fifth lockout bypass baking in a shape M8 P2 will restructure.
  - **M8 DECLARATION DUTY, and every header and handoff in this batch must state it verbatim: "adds no replicated property, no new replicated class, no new relevancy tier."** "Tier not declared" is a QA FAIL, and "there is nothing to declare" only counts when it is stated.
  - **🚩 `Capture(World, ETeamId::Red)` RETURNS CORRECT TOTALS BUT ALL-ZERO ORDERABLE/FOLLOWABLE COLUMNS — FLAGGED, NOT FIXED, AND M8 P2 MUST NOT INHERIT THIS AS A SURPRISE (found at TASK-416, 2026-08-02).** Both shipped eligibility predicates — `IsFollowCommandEligible()` and `IsGroupCommandEligible()` — **hardcode `Team == ETeamId::Blue`**. So the snapshot's `ETeamId` parameter **implies a generality the columns do not have.** This is **correct for v1** (host/standalone, Blue is the only commanding player) and it was deliberately **NOT "fixed"**, because fixing it in the assistant lane would mean **reimplementing predicates that are banned from reimplementation** — the Cleric-follows-but-cannot-hold split has exactly one owner. **The parameter is kept** (removing it would bake the Blue assumption into the signature, which is worse). ⚠️ **When M8 P2 makes Red a real commanding player, the fix belongs in the SHIPPED predicates, not here** — and every caller then works unchanged.
- **EXECUTOR SEAM (Wave 1, named here so no implementer invents it):** `Attack`/`Defend` are **latched army-wide stances with no selection** — "send 10 footmen at the castle" is **not** the Attack stance. So selection-bearing verbs (`send`/`guard`/`ambush`) stay separate from army-wide ones (`charge`/`fallback`): `send`/`guard` → `CreateUnitGroup(Hold, …)` · `ambush` → `CreateUnitGroup(Ambush, …)` · `follow` → `EnrollInDefaultFollowGroup(Unit)` (already public, already idempotent) · `charge`/`fallback` → `SetUnitCommand(Attack/Defend)` · `rally` → `AHeroCharacter::Rally()`. Selector resolution filters on the **shipping** eligibility predicates `IsFollowCommandEligible()` / `IsGroupCommandEligible()` — **never reimplemented** (the Cleric-follows-but-cannot-hold split is already law) — sorts **nearest to the TARGET, not to the hero**, and **on shortfall returns it, never truncates silently.**

### 9. PINNED CROSS-TASK SIGNATURE REGISTRY (the contract that makes the parallel tasks link)
⚠️ **UBT compiles the whole module.** Every task in this batch compiles against this list **character-for-character**; a task that "improves" a pinned signature breaks the link and is an **automatic QA FAIL**. Access levels are part of the pin. **Several of these tasks CANNOT COMPILE ALONE and are not expected to** — they compile together at the batch's integration compile (the TASK-395/396 precedent). That is designed, not a defect; do not open a QA loop over it.

```cpp
// ── Plugins/SiegeLlama — module SiegeLlama ────────────────────────────────
// SiegeLlamaSubsystem.h
DECLARE_LOG_CATEGORY_EXTERN(LogSiegeLlama, Log, All);
DECLARE_DELEGATE_ThreeParams(FSiegeLlamaCompletionSignature, bool /*bSuccess*/, const FString& /*Output*/, const FString& /*Error*/);

UCLASS()
class SIEGELLAMA_API USiegeLlamaSubsystem : public UGameInstanceSubsystem
{
public:
    bool IsReady() const;                       // model loaded, worker alive, not faulted
    bool IsBusy() const;                        // a request is in flight (queue depth 1)
    // Returns false immediately when !IsReady() or IsBusy(). OnComplete ALWAYS fires on the GAME THREAD.
    bool RequestCompletion(const FString& Prompt, const FString& Grammar, const FSiegeLlamaCompletionSignature& OnComplete);
    void CancelActiveRequest();                 // routes through llama abort_callback
};

// ── Source/GitClaudeUnrealTest/Siegebound — game lane ─────────────────────
// SiegeAssistantCommand.h  (pure-data header; the UnitCommand.h / TeamId.h precedent)
DECLARE_LOG_CATEGORY_EXTERN(LogSiegeAssistant, Log, All);

UENUM()
enum class ESiegeAssistantIntent : uint8 { None, Send, Guard, Ambush, Follow, Charge, Fallback, Rally };

// ⚠️ THE SELECTION IS MULTI-KIND. CORRECTED 2026-08-02 (manager) — the first pin
// carried a SINGULAR `FName Kind`, which could not express the feature's own
// flagship sentence, "send 10 footmen WITH A SORCERER to the nearest ancient
// ground". The approved plan is internally inconsistent here: its GBNF sketch
// shows `kind` as a RULE (singular by nature) but its go/no-go bar #5 scores
// exact-match on "{intent, KINDS, COUNTS, where}" — plural. Plural is the intent;
// the sketch simply never drew the top-level object. Found while specifying the
// evaluation corpus's expected outputs, which is exactly what that task is for.
static constexpr int32 SiegeAssistantMaxSelectionKinds = 3;

USTRUCT()
struct FSiegeAssistantCommand      // uint8 / int32 / FName ONLY — M8 P2 takes this as-is over the wire
{
    ESiegeAssistantIntent Intent = ESiegeAssistantIntent::None;
    // INDEX-ALIGNED PARALLEL ARRAYS, never an array of structs — that is what keeps
    // the payload to FName/int32 only and preserves the "P2 takes it as-is" property
    // (~30 bytes at the cap). Kinds.Num() == Counts.Num() is an invariant the parser
    // enforces; a mismatch is a parse failure, never a silent truncation.
    TArray<FName> Kinds;                 // canonical unit symbols, e.g. "footman"; HARD CAP SiegeAssistantMaxSelectionKinds
    TArray<int32> Counts;                // 0 == "all"; grammar range is 1..30 (NOT the live max)
    FName Where           = NAME_None;   // canonical place symbol
    FName TriggerKind     = NAME_None;   // deferred-intent trigger; NAME_None == "now"
    int32 TriggerAtLeast  = 0;
};

// SiegeAssistantCommand.h — free function, NOT a member
bool ParseSiegeAssistantCommand(const FString& Json, FSiegeAssistantCommand& OutCommand, FString& OutError);

// SiegeAssistantVocabulary.h
USTRUCT(BlueprintType)
struct FSiegeAssistantSynonym { FName Canonical = NAME_None; TArray<FString> Aliases; };

UCLASS(BlueprintType)
class USiegeAssistantVocabulary : public UDataAsset
{
public:
    UPROPERTY(EditDefaultsOnly, Category = "Siegebound|Assistant") TArray<FSiegeAssistantSynonym> UnitSynonyms;
    UPROPERTY(EditDefaultsOnly, Category = "Siegebound|Assistant") TArray<FSiegeAssistantSynonym> PlaceSynonyms;
    UPROPERTY(EditDefaultsOnly, Category = "Siegebound|Assistant") TArray<FSiegeAssistantSynonym> IntentSynonyms;
    FString BuildSynonymTable() const;   // the Zone-A synonym block, deterministic ordering
};

// SiegeAssistantGrammar.h — PURE + DETERMINISTIC: no UWorld, no engine state, no UObject inputs.
// This is deliberate — it is what makes automation tests cheap and what decouples A4 from A3.
// Emits a SELECTION LIST of 1..SiegeAssistantMaxSelectionKinds {kind, count} pairs
// (see the FSiegeAssistantCommand correction above). The cap is IN THE GRAMMAR, not
// only in the parser: an unbounded repetition rule is precisely what a small model
// rambles into, and a bounded alternation costs nothing.
UCLASS()
class USiegeAssistantGrammar : public UObject
{
public:
    static FString Build(const TArray<FName>& UnitKinds, const TArray<FName>& PlaceNames);
};

// SiegeAssistantSnapshot.h
USTRUCT()
struct FSiegeAssistantRosterEntry { FName Kind = NAME_None; int32 Count = 0; int32 GroupId = INDEX_NONE; };

UCLASS()
class USiegeAssistantSnapshot : public UObject
{
public:
    void Capture(UWorld* World, ETeamId Team);                       // six TActorIterator passes, per SENTENCE, never per tick
    FString BuildZoneA(const USiegeAssistantVocabulary* Vocabulary) const;   // STATIC — byte-identical for the life of the process
    FString BuildZoneB() const;                                      // SLOW  — quantized match facts
    FString BuildZoneC(const FString& Utterance, const FString& PendingLine) const;  // FAST
    const TArray<FSiegeAssistantRosterEntry>& GetRoster() const;
    const TArray<FName>& GetPlaceNames() const;
    const TArray<FName>& GetUnitKinds() const;
    bool ResolvePlace(FName Place, FVector& OutLocation) const;      // resolved FVectors stay GAME-SIDE, never in the prompt
};

// AncientGround.h — AAncientGround, public static (the AGoldNode::FindBestMineFor idiom)
static AAncientGround* FindNearestAncientGround(UWorld* World, const FVector& From);
```

#### 9a. ⚖️ THE v1 PLACE VOCABULARY — PINNED CHARACTER-FOR-CHARACTER (added 2026-08-02)
**Nothing pinned a symbol for the mine, so TASK-426's corpus author had to choose one and flagged it rather than assuming.** They were right to flag: **a mismatch here fails the go/no-go for a NAMING reason rather than a MODEL reason, which is the worst way to fail a gate** — it reads as "the model is bad" when the model was fine. The seven symbols, exact spelling, lowercase, snake_case:

```
enemy_castle · own_castle · mid · ancient_ground_near · ancient_ground_far · nearest_mine · hero
```

- **⚠️ IT IS `own_castle`, NOT `my_castle` — AND THE STORY IS THE LESSON, SO IT IS RECORDED.** A `my_castle` spelling circulated briefly in an orchestrator message as a **transcription error** and was **retracted by the orchestrator the same day**. It was caught twice independently, both times the same way: **by verifying against the artifact on disk instead of trusting the message.** TASK-416's agent checked the corpus files and shipped `own_castle`; the manager checked and overruled the pin. `my_castle` **appears nowhere on disk.** The sealed corpus asserts `own_castle` in BOTH files — `DEV-16` and, decisively, **`HOLD-06`, which is in the holdout set that scores bar #5** — and under §11 the holdout is **frozen**, so a rename would either void the accuracy number or fail the gate for a **naming reason rather than a model reason.** `own_castle` is also what §5/§8 already said *before* the corpus was authored. **Standing lesson: a pinned symbol is verified against the file that asserts it, never against the message that quotes it.** `own_castle` stands; do not "modernize" it.
- **`nearest_mine`** — adopted from TASK-426 verbatim; it matches the shipped `AGoldNode::FindBestMineFor` idiom and the near/far derivation style. Asserted by `DEV-23` and `HOLD-12`.
- **`hero`** — pinned for completeness (Follow and Rally both anchor on the hero). ⚠️ **NO corpus row asserts it**, so it is *not* exercised by the go/no-go gate; it is forward-looking vocabulary, not measured coverage. Recorded so nobody reads its presence as tested.
- **This list is CLOSED for v1.** Widening it is a later pass **with its own accuracy re-measurement** — adding a place silently changes Zone A, which invalidates both the KV-prefix claim and the measured accuracy.
  - ⚖️ **AND, ADDED 2026-08-03, THAT LATER PASS ALSO OWES A CONTEXT-BUDGET RE-CHECK — THIS SENTENCE IS THE SANCTIONED DOOR INTO §8's ZONE-A SIZE BOUND, WHICH IS WHY THE DUTY IS WRITTEN HERE AND NOT ONLY THERE.** Widening the vocabulary **grows Zone A**, Zone A is bounded in size by `n_ctx_actual` (§8 ⚖️ THE ZONE-A SIZE BOUND), and today's slack is **~413 tokens** — finite, and this is the clause most likely to spend it. So a vocabulary-widening pass re-runs **`Siege.Llama.SpikePrompt` for the new Zone-A token count** and re-checks the startup budget assertion **before** it is considered done. ⚠️ **An accuracy re-measurement alone would not catch an overflow** — it would catch its *symptom*, mis-attributed to the model, which is exactly the failure the size bound exists to prevent.

#### 9c. ⚠️ THE ZONE-A ↔ GRAMMAR SEAM — THE PROMPT'S FEW-SHOTS AND THE GBNF ARE ONE CONTRACT, AND NOTHING ENFORCES IT (added 2026-08-02)
**This is a SILENT-FAILURE seam, and silent-failure seams are what have cost this project most.** The few-shot examples in Zone A teach the model a JSON shape; the GBNF *forces* a JSON shape. **If they disagree, constrained decoding fights the few-shots on every token and accuracy collapses for a reason NO LOG LINE NAMES** — the model looks stupid, the grammar looks fine, and the go/no-go bar fails for a cause nobody can see. Today the entire tie between them is a code comment.

**How we know: TASK-416 wrote Zone A from the approved plan's GBNF sketch, then read TASK-417's LANDED files and found the sketch wrong in four ways.** The plan's sketch is **not** the schema — the landed grammar is. The four, recorded so nobody re-derives them from the sketch:
| Sketch said | Landed truth |
|---|---|
| selection key `select` | **`who`** |
| per-pair count key `count` | **`n`** — `count` is the GBNF *rule* name, never a JSON key |
| army-wide verb emits `[]` | **`"who":"none"`** |
| (absent) | a **`{"ask":ASK}`** question branch the few-shots must teach |

- **THE LAW:** the JSON key names are **owned by the landed grammar (TASK-417) and by nothing else.** Zone A's few-shots, the parser, and any documentation **conform to it** — a disagreement is resolved by changing the prompt, never by changing the grammar to match a doc.
- **⚖️ QA CRITERION, EXPLICIT, AT TASK-412 AND TASK-419: every few-shot example in Zone A must be VALIDATED AGAINST THE LANDED GBNF** — parse each few-shot's JSON with the same grammar/parser the runtime uses and show it accepts. **"It looks like the schema" is not evidence.** A few-shot the grammar would reject is a **BLOCKER**, not a warning.
- **The plan's GBNF sketch is superseded on sight by the landed grammar.** If a spec, a handoff or this document quotes a key name that the landed grammar does not emit, the landed grammar wins and the quote is a defect to fix.
- **⚠️ THE SAME SEAM ONE LEVEL DOWN — THE SHAPE IS PINNED, THE SYMBOLS ARE NOT: ZONE A TEACHES THREE UNIT KINDS THE GRAMMAR MAY FORBID (added 2026-08-02, `qa/TASK-412.md` NIT-4).** Everything above governs the *shape* — which keys exist. The **values** have the identical failure and, until now, no owner: the landed `BuildZoneA` names **`footman` / `sorcerer` / `archer` unconditionally** (`SiegeAssistantSnapshot.cpp:666-669`), while the landed grammar generates its `kind` alternatives **from the live roster** (`SiegeAssistantGrammar.cpp:250-262`, whose own comment is *"every alternative here is a unit that actually exists right now; anything else is unreachable for the sampler"*). **On a board where any one of those three kinds is absent, Zone A demonstrates — in its only two command few-shots — a symbol the sampler physically forbids.**
  - **What makes it dangerous is an absence, not an event: THERE IS NO LOG LINE.** Nothing throws, nothing refuses, nothing warns, and the JSON that does come back is well-formed. The model is pulled toward a token constrained decoding silently declines to allow, so the cost arrives as **diffuse accuracy loss with no cause attached** — spread across rows that have nothing to do with the missing kind, and read as *"the model is bad"*. That is the §9c failure shape exactly, which is why it is recorded here rather than as a footnote elsewhere.
  - **⚠️ THE SPIKE CANNOT SEE IT — THE FIXTURE MASKS IT.** TASK-410's bench fields **13 kinds including all three**, so every few-shot symbol is always present in the generated grammar. **No measurement in this batch can surface this, and a green bar #5 is NOT evidence that this seam is closed.**
  - **The obvious fix is BARRED.** Generating the few-shots from the live roster would make **Zone A state-dependent** — §8 calls that a defect and it destroys the KV prefix outright, a strictly worse failure than the one being repaired. The close therefore has to come from the other side: either the demonstrated symbols are guaranteed admissible, or the few-shots demonstrate with kinds that cannot be absent. **Whoever owns the fix picks; whoever "fixes" it by making Zone A dynamic has broken the larger law.**
  - **🔍 THE SIGNATURE SHARED BY ALL THREE SEAMS RECORDED IN THIS BATCH** — this one, §8's `MaxRosterKinds` collapse, and §10's requested-vs-actual context — **is: no crash, no error, no log line, and in two of the three a number that still looks fine.** That defect class has dominated this batch, and every instance was found by reading the landed artifact rather than the document describing it.

- **⛔ A DUMP IS NOT A PARSE — AND THIS SECTION'S OWN VERIFICATION METHOD IS WHAT MISSED IT (added 2026-08-03; `handoffs/TASK-413-buildmaster.md` PART 2 §13).**
  > **String-comparing two generators can never prove either is valid — only the target parser can.** Any grammar shipped to llama.cpp must be **PARSED BY llama.cpp** before it is believed. Mirroring, diffing and by-derivation review are **necessary and NOT SUFFICIENT**.

  `llama_sampler_init_grammar` returned **NULL on every generation of all six bench runs.** The cause is one character: **`at_least` is not a legal GBNF rule name.** llama.cpp reads a rule name as **letters, digits and hyphens only — an underscore TERMINATES the name** — so the parser takes `at`, demands `::=`, finds `_least`, and rejects the entire grammar (`parse: error parsing grammar: expecting ::= at _least ::= "1" | "2" | ...`). llama.cpp's own grammars are kebab-case throughout. ⚠️ **The defect is in the AUTHORITY this section names — `SiegeAssistantGrammar.cpp:312` (the rule) and `:392` (the reference) — not merely in the spike's mirror.** The rename is `at_least` → **`at-least`**, rule identifier only.
  - **THE JSON KEY `"at_least"` IS CORRECT AND STAYS.** It lives inside a **string terminal** (`"\"at_least\":"`), where the rule-name charset does not apply. It is the wire format, and TASK-426's **sealed** corpus asserts it — renaming the key to "match" would be a corpus breach dressed as a fix. **Rule names and JSON keys are different alphabets; that is the whole trap.**
  - **THE BUILDER NOW CARRIES A VALIDATOR THAT REJECTS AN ILLEGAL RULE NAME AT CONSTRUCTION.** The class is closed in the authority rather than the instance fixed once — a future rule named with an underscore fails loudly where it is written, not silently where it is loaded.
  - **⚠️ WHY THIS BELONGS IN §9c AND NOWHERE ELSE: §9c exists to police this seam, and its verification method until now was *diff the two generators*. QA did exactly that, correctly, and reported them identical rule-for-rule. THEY ARE IDENTICAL — THEY ARE IDENTICALLY UNPARSEABLE.** Part 1 of the same spike diffed the runtime dump against the landed generator and blessed the seam as holding at runtime. **Two of the most careful reviews of this batch both passed a grammar that had never once loaded.** A method that compares an artifact against another artifact of the same authorship **cannot detect an error they share** — and a shared error is the likely one, because they were written from the same head.
  - **THE FAILURE WAS SILENT IN EXACTLY THE SHAPE THIS BATCH KEEPS PRODUCING — AND NOTE PRECISELY WHAT IT COST.** A NULL sampler is not a refusal: generation **continues, unconstrained**. So bars #2 (latency for ~60 *constrained* tokens) and #5 (accuracy) were **NOT MEASURED — not measured wrongly.** In-engine with the grammar dead, **every warm CPU iteration emitted `<think>` prose and burned the whole budget to the 10 s abort** (206–480 output tokens, zero JSON, every tier); with the fixed grammar the same prompt at `--temp 0` returned `{"intent":"fallback","who":"none","where":"mid","when":"now"}`. ⇒ **The grammar is LOAD-BEARING, not a safety net:** it structurally forbids `<think>` from token 0 (both `root` alternatives open with a `{` literal). **The only reason this run is reportable at all is that the harness logged the NULL at `Error` level and refused to score against it.**
  - **⚖️ ACCEPTANCE CRITERION, from here on: any task that authors or edits the GBNF pastes a PARSE into its handoff** — a clean load by the **vendored build** (b10235, the same binaries the plugin links; `llama-completion.exe` offline or a non-NULL `llama_sampler_init_grammar` at runtime). **A dump is not evidence. A diff is not evidence. "It matches the generator" is not evidence.** A grammar that parses under some *other* llama.cpp release is not evidence either.
  - **⚖️ AND IT REFINES THE SIGNATURE ONE BULLET ABOVE — PROMOTED 2026-08-03 TO GENERAL LAW. THIS IS NOT COMMENTARY ON ONE GRAMMAR.**
    > **A method that compares an artifact to another artifact of the SAME AUTHORSHIP cannot detect an error they SHARE — and a shared error is the LIKELY one, because both were written from the same head.**
    > **Reading the artifact is the FLOOR, not the ceiling. Where an artifact has a PARSER, THE PARSER IS THE REVIEWER OF RECORD.**

    The three seams recorded one bullet above were each found **by reading the landed artifact** instead of the document describing it. **This one could not have been, and that is the whole lesson: THE LANDED ARTIFACT READ CORRECTLY.** It was well-formed, self-consistent, and matched every document describing it — so **re-reading could never have found it**, however careful the reader and however many readers there were. Two of this batch's most careful reviews both passed it. **It took EXECUTION.**
    - **The law binds every artifact on this project that has a runnable consumer, not just GBNF:** GBNF → llama.cpp · the eval CSVs → TASK-410's runner · `.uplugin`/`.uproject`/`.ini` → their loaders · `.gitignore` → **`git check-ignore -v`** (§7's *"ACCEPTANCE, NOT ASSERTION"* is this same law already written down for one file — it is the pattern being generalised, not a separate rule) · Python → import/compile · C++ → the compiler. **`FString::Printf` format strings and `DataTable` row schemas have consumers too.**
    - **⚖️ WHAT A REVIEWER OWES WHEN THE PARSER WAS NOT RUN: say so.** "Reviewed by reading" is a legitimate, useful, INCOMPLETE review — and it must be **reported as incomplete**, naming the parser that was not run. **Reporting it as a PASS is how a grammar that had never once loaded reached a measurement run**, and the only reason that run is reportable at all is that the harness logged the NULL at `Error` and refused to score against it.

#### 9b. ⛔ NAMED ALIAS PROHIBITION — `mage` STAYS UNALIASED, PERMANENTLY
**`mage` must resolve to NOTHING.** `sorcerer` and `wizard` are **different cards** — the Sorcerer is the non-attacking ley-warden empowerer, the Wizard is the AoE fireball caster — and they must share **no alias whatsoever**. The sealed corpus carries `the mage` in **both** files (`DEV-06`, `HOLD-09`) as a **`Clarify`** row.

**Aliasing `mage` to either card converts an honest clarification into a confident wrong command — the exact valid-shaped-wrong-command failure this feature's entire architecture exists to prevent.** This is written as a **named prohibition rather than a note** because *"add the obvious alias"* is precisely what a future task will do, and it would look like a helpful improvement while silently deleting a gate row's meaning. **Adding a `mage` alias is an automatic QA FAIL**, in this batch and in every later one.

### 10. TUNABLES (all `EditDefaultsOnly` / named constants, ALL flagged for Jonathan's feel pass)
`SoftTimeoutSeconds` **4.0** · `HardTimeoutSeconds` **10.0** · `MaxOutputTokens` **96** · `MaxSnapshotChars` **1085** (⚠️ **governs ZONE B + ZONE C ONLY, never Zone A** — = the ≤400-token cap at the **MEASURED 2.71** chars/token of that region, `Qwen3-4B-Q4_K_M`. ⚖️ **RULED DOWN FROM 1440 on 2026-08-03 from `Siege.Llama.SpikePrompt`'s printed counts — §8's TRIGGER fired: 1440 admitted 531 tok against a 400-token budget, over-admitting by ~33 %. CONDITIONAL AND THE CONDITION IS PART OF THE RULING: it holds only while the truncation the tighter cap now performs is OBSERVABLY LOGGED** — WARN-5 already showed this builder's collapse path is silent, and a tighter cap that silently degrades the prompt is worse than a loose one that does not. ⛔ **"Corrected from the spike's real measurement" stood here falsely until 2026-08-02 and was WITHDRAWN; that history stands — the phrase is true only from 2026-08-03, and only for this tokenizer.** As-built B+C = **955 chars = 352 tok — BOTH MEASURED — i.e. 88 % of the token budget while the char reading advertised 66 %**; see §8 🔢 TOKEN PROVENANCE, and note **2.13 ⇒ ~850** is the stricter bound if Zone C ever becomes as symbol-dense as Zone B. ⚖️ **ROLE-LABELLED 2026-08-03 — VALUE UNCHANGED AT 1085: THIS IS THE *AUTHORITY* VALUE, AND IT IS RETIRED, NOT RE-POINTED, AT TASK-423's COMMIT.** It is correct because it assumes the **smallest** plausible ratio; a **pre-filter** must assume the **largest**, so the safe direction inverts with the role. TASK-423 enforces the budget by **tokenizing** (`MaxSnapshotTokens` **400**) and introduces a **separate** constant **`SnapshotPreFilterMaxChars` = 3000 chars** (floor **1424** = `400 × 3.56`, the measured whole-prompt ratio; generous by ruling — erring high costs one wasted tokenize, erring low silently truncates). **After that commit `MaxSnapshotChars` does not exist.** ⛔ **Do NOT carry 1085 into the pre-filter under any name — full ruling: §8 resolution (2).** ⇒ ✅ **CONDITION STATUS + RE-ANCHORING (2026-08-03, §8's EIGHTH SEAM).** The observable-truncation condition above **is LANDED — the post-TASK-413 pass shipped the log in the same pass as the cap and closed WARN-5's `<`-boundary bug (it now fires on *"did any kind fail to print in full"*, names the cause bit, and escalates on every deepening) — UNDER QA, NOT VERIFIED.** ⛔ **AND THE CONDITION DOES *NOT* DIE WITH THIS CONSTANT.** It was written as a rider on the cap; it is **re-anchored onto THE ACT**, and now binds **any enforcement of the snapshot budget, by any mechanism, under any name** — `MaxSnapshotTokens`, `SnapshotPreFilterMaxChars`, or anything that later replaces them. **A subsystem that truncates the snapshot without an observable log has not implemented the clause, regardless of which bound it enforced** — and TASK-423's **new** cut sites are not covered by the snapshot builder's existing log. ⚠️ **TASK-423 ALSO OWES §10 THE TOKEN CONSTANT ITSELF: `MaxSnapshotTokens` = 400 is pinned in §8 as "value already law, §10", but §10 carries the 400 only as an OPERAND of the char derivation above — so retiring `MaxSnapshotChars` would otherwise delete the budget's only home in this section. It lands here as a real named tunable in the same pass**) · `GrammarCountMax` **30** (⚠️ NOT the live roster max — see §1) · `SiegeAssistantMaxSelectionKinds` **3** (⚠️ a `static constexpr`, NOT a tunable — it is pinned in §9 and enforced in BOTH the grammar and the parser) · `DeferredIntentTTLSeconds` **120** · `DeferredIntentPollHz` **1** (armed only while latched) · `ContextTokens` **2048** (⚖️ **FROZEN AT 2048 UNTIL AFTER THE RE-RUN — ruled 2026-08-03; raising it would invalidate bars #1/#3/#4, which have already PASSED at this value. See §8 ⚖️ THE ZONE-A SIZE BOUND: the guardrail lands first, the number moves later, on measurement, on Jonathan's call**) · `GpuLayers` **-1 = auto-tier** (full-offload / partial / CPU-only, chosen from the spike's numbers).
- **⚠️ `ContextTokens` — AND ANY BATCH SIZE — IS A *REQUESTED* VALUE. THE CONTEXT IS ENTITLED TO USE A DIFFERENT ONE, AND THE VENDORED HEADER SAYS SO IN AS MANY WORDS (added 2026-08-02; `llama.h:551-556`, `qa/TASK-412.md` WARN-2).** *"After creating a llama_context, it is recommended to query the actual values using these functions … the requested values via llama_context_params may differ from the actual values used by the context."* The actuals are **`llama_n_ctx(ctx)` / `llama_n_batch(ctx)` / `llama_n_ubatch(ctx)`**, and **anything that sizes work reads those, never the tunable.** **Why this earns a clause instead of a comment: if `n_batch` comes back clamped, a prefill slice sized from the requested value is bigger than the context will accept, `llama_decode` rejects it outright, and EVERY run reads "inference failed" — a total-failure mode that presents as a model problem while the `.ini` value that caused it looks perfectly correct.** The same applies to a clamped `n_ctx` against §8's prompt-budget pre-check: the budget is checked against the **actual** context size or it is not checked. **The spike's copy is being corrected under WARN-2 — ⚠️ but TASK-423 INHERITS THE IDIOM:** the shipped subsystem queries both actuals at load, budgets from them, and **logs requested and actual side by side once** so a clamp is visible rather than inferred. **The tunable stays the request; the actual is what is enforced against.** ⇒ **MEASURED 2026-08-03: the assembled prompt is 1504 tok, so with `MaxOutputTokens` 96 reserved the feature occupies 1600 of a REQUESTED 2048 — 78 %.** ⇒ ⚖️ **THE HEADROOM QUESTION THAT FIGURE RAISED IS NOW DECIDED (2026-08-03), AND IT WENT TO THE GUARDRAIL, NOT TO THE NUMBER: `ContextTokens` STAYS 2048 before the re-run — bars #1/#3/#4 already passed at it and `n_ctx` drives KV-cache size and prefill cost, so moving it would silently invalidate three passing measurements to fix a problem that is not yet biting.** The bound instead is **TASK-423's MANDATORY startup budget assertion** — `ZoneA_tokens + MaxSnapshotTokens + MaxOutputTokens + safety_margin ≤ llama_n_ctx(ctx)`, computed **after the context exists**, failing **loudly at load** and naming which operand overflowed. **Full ruling, operands and headroom arithmetic: §8 ⚖️ THE ZONE-A SIZE BOUND.** ⚠️ **This clause is the reason the assertion may not read the tunable:** an assertion that checks the *requested* 2048 is not a check at all on the exact machine where a clamp makes it matter.

**Compile traps — restate in every dispatch touching these files:** no literal `*/` inside doc comments · `FString::Printf` format strings must be literal/`constexpr` (UE 5.8 `TCheckedFormatString`, the TASK-268 C7595 lesson) · the no-shadowing law · the complete-type include law.

### 11. ⚠️ THE EVALUATION CORPUS IS SEALED, SPLIT, AND AUTHORED BY A DIFFERENT OWNER THAN THE PROMPT
Added 2026-08-02 at the orchestrator's direction, closing a self-grading risk the manager escalated. **Accuracy bar #5 is the go/no-go number for a feature that could consume weeks — it must not be scored against a test set written by the person tuning the prompt.** This project has been burned by exactly this shape three times already: `Build.bat` returning exit 0 on a failed build, `rig_character.py` silently ignoring an override that did not exist, and MCP readback passing on visually-broken UMG. **Every one produced a confident green that was not real.**

- **DIFFERENT OWNER, STRUCTURALLY.** The corpus is authored by a **different task and a different role** than the prompt/few-shot author. Not an honour system — two dispatches, two contexts.
- **SEALED BEFORE THE PROMPT EXISTS.** The corpus task lands **before** the prompt is written or tuned, so the prompt cannot be fitted to it and the corpus cannot be narrowed to what the prompt already handles. **Fitting direction matters:** corpus → vocabulary/prompt *coverage* is legitimate requirements flow; vocabulary/prompt → corpus is the cheat.
- **⚖️ SPLIT 40 = 25 DEV + 15 HOLDOUT, TWO FILES.** The plan's own remediation ladder for a missed accuracy bar begins *"better few-shots"* — which **is** tuning against the eval. So few-shot tuning happens against **DEV only**; the **HOLDOUT is opened exactly once, at the measurement run, and the HOLDOUT number is the one reported against the ≥ 85 % bar.** A dev-set number quoted as the bar is a QA FAIL.
- **THE FEW-SHOTS MUST BE DISJOINT FROM BOTH FILES** — no corpus sentence may appear as a Zone-A few-shot. QA checks this by literal string comparison, not by assertion.
- **ADVERSARIAL BY REQUIREMENT, not by good intentions.** Mandatory coverage: ambiguous quantities · units that do not exist in the live roster · **the Sorcerer/Wizard collision** (different cards; a shared alias makes the assistant confidently command the wrong unit) · phrasings that mix a **selection-bearing** verb with an **army-wide** one · multi-kind selections · deferred phrasings · and rows whose correct outcome is **Clarify** or **Refuse**, not Execute.
- **CANONICAL HOME + SCHEMA (pinned):** `Docs/Data/assistant_eval_dev.csv` and `Docs/Data/assistant_eval_holdout.csv`, siblings of `cards.csv` (the project's data-source-of-truth home). Identical header, character-for-character:
  ```
  Id,Sentence,ExpectOutcome,ExpectKinds,ExpectCounts,ExpectWhere,ExpectTriggerKind,ExpectTriggerAtLeast,Notes
  ```
  `ExpectOutcome` ∈ `Execute` | `Clarify` | `Refuse`. `ExpectKinds` / `ExpectCounts` are **`|`-separated and index-aligned** (the `FSiegeAssistantCommand` shape). Empty cell = the field is not asserted for that row.
- **⚖️ INTENT IS ENCODED IN `Notes`, AND THAT ENCODING IS ACCEPTED LAW (ruling 2026-08-02).** The pinned header has **no `ExpectIntent` column**, but bar #5 scores `{intent, kinds, counts, where}` — and rows like `charge with the footmen` vs `send the footmen at their castle` differ in **nothing but intent**. TASK-426 hit this and **did the right thing: it did NOT unilaterally add a 10th column** (that would have broken the contract TASK-410's parser is written against, silently, after the seal). Instead every `Notes` cell is prefixed **`intent=<value>; `**, matched by **`^intent=([a-z]+); `** — the 7 intents plus **`none`** (Refuse rows) and **`unasserted`** (where the verb itself is the ambiguity). **Accepted because the runner scores it with a one-line regex and ZERO corpus edit, and — decisively — the values were authored before any prompt existed, so the seal holds.** The regex is part of the pinned contract: TASK-410's runner parses it, and the `Notes` prefix may not be reformatted.
- **🚩 KNOWN LIMITATION, v1-ACCEPTED, RECORDED SO NOBODY MISREADS IT: an empty cell is OVERLOADED.** It means *"not asserted"*, so a row whose correct answer is *"this field IS empty"* (`rally`, `fallback` — no kinds, no place) **cannot be positively asserted**; it is carried in `Notes` prose today. ⚠️ **Never read an empty cell as a passing assertion** — it is scored as *skipped*, not as *matched*. Fixing it properly means a sentinel value (e.g. `<none>`) in a future corpus revision, which is a **new** corpus, never an edit to the sealed one.
- **⚠️ THE CORPUS IS LOADED BY PATH AT RUNTIME, NEVER `#include`d INTO THE PLUGIN.** Baking Siegebound sentences into `Plugins/SiegeLlama/` would give the plugin a Siegebound dependency it is architecturally forbidden to have (§8's lane split). The paths are passed on the console command line.
- **REACTIVE COVERAGE IS DECLARED, NOT HIDDEN.** If a later vocabulary or prompt task adds an alias or a few-shot *because a corpus row needed it*, its handoff **lists them** — legitimate work, but the reader must know the coverage was reactive.
- **🔍 AND THE SAME INSTINCT GOVERNS EVERY CLAIM IN THIS BATCH: a relayed diagnosis is a LEAD, not a finding.** See **"🔍 THE RELAYED-DIAGNOSIS LAW (2026-08-02)"** above. This batch alone produced three relayed root causes that were wrong or incomplete — the `my_castle` pin, the `C2228`/`C2737` "include law" reading, and the obvious-but-shallow `C4172` read. **Every one was caught by checking the artifact instead of the message.** The corpus's whole reason for existing is the same principle applied to a benchmark: **do not grade yourself from your own report.**

### 12. ⚖️ THE ACCURACY LADDER WAVE — the post-spike remediation law (added 2026-08-03, manager, on Jonathan's TASK-415 rulings)

**Context, stated once so no clause below has to re-argue it.** The spike measured all six bars (`handoffs/TASK-413-buildmaster.md` PART 3). Bars #1/#3/#4/#6 PASS. **Bar #5 FAILED at 66.7 % against 85 %**, with `parse_failures = 0` on all 40 rows and `STRICT == LENIENT` — ⇒ **the grammar provably works and the failure is COMPREHENSION, not FORMAT.** Constrained decoding was never going to fix comprehension.

**Jonathan's two rulings (DECIDED 2026-08-03 — no agent re-opens them):**
1. **ITERATE THE PROMPT LADDER FIRST** — the plan's own rungs **1 (better few-shots)** then **2 (vocabulary)**, then **re-measure**. ⛔ **Not a bigger model, not fine-tuning, not a kill.** Rung 3 and beyond are HIS call, at a gate, on a number.
2. **DIAGNOSE THE CPU ABORTS BEFORE ANYONE CHANGES THE CPU-FALLBACK REQUIREMENT.** His ruling (i) — CPU fallback required — **stands until he moves it.** The question to answer is whether the 2-of-5 truncated CPU generations are a **TUNABLE** (a deadline / token budget cutting generation short) or a **WALL**. ⛔ **Diagnose, then bring a recommendation. Do not quietly redefine the requirement.**

#### 12a. 🔒 THE SPENT-SEAL LAW + HOLDOUT GENERATIONS

> **A holdout is SPENT the moment its rows are scored AND any per-row result reaches a report, a handoff or the board. A spent holdout is NEVER scored again — every subsequent score against it would be fitted.**

- **`assistant_eval_holdout.csv` (generation 1) IS SPENT as of 2026-08-03.** Its five failing rows are printed in `handoffs/TASK-413-buildmaster.md` §21a and on the board, and they are therefore **burned as tuning signal**. It is **never re-scored, never edited, and never deleted** — it stays in git as the record of what generation 1 measured.
- **THE DEV SPLIT IS *NOT* SPENT AND IS NOT GENERATIONAL.** `Docs/Data/assistant_eval_dev.csv` is what tuning is scored against, freely and repeatedly. **A dev number is a fitted number by construction and is never the go/no-go figure** (unchanged from §11).
- **NAMING LAW — HOLDOUT GENERATIONS:** `Docs/Data/assistant_eval_holdout<N>.csv`, `N` incrementing from **2**. Generation 1 keeps its historical un-suffixed name. Each generation:
  - is authored by **an owner who did no tuning** (§11's structural rule, and see 12b);
  - carries the **pinned 9-column header character-for-character** and the `^intent=([a-z]+); ` `Notes` prefix contract, unchanged;
  - is **15 rows**, authored against the **FROZEN `t0` fixture** in `SiegeLlamaSpike.cpp` (a row whose truth depends on a live count is only true at t0; **t0's bytes may not be changed to suit a corpus row** — that silently rewrites every earlier premise and moves bar #3's zone boundaries);
  - is **SEALED at handoff and opened exactly once**, by the measurement task, on `tier=full` (the only tier with zero aborts — an aborted generation scores as a wrong answer and corrupts the number).
- **ZERO CODE CHANGE IS REQUIRED TO ADOPT A GENERATION.** `Siege.Llama.SpikeEval` takes `holdout=<path>` and **deliberately has no default**. A new generation is a new file and a new command line.
- ⛔ **GENERATIONS ARE NOT ROW-COMPARABLE, AND A GENERATION-TO-GENERATION DELTA IS NOT EVIDENCE.** Each generation is authored to fix the weaknesses the last one revealed about *itself* (generation 1's own printed degenerate floor was **60.0 %**, leaving its 66.7 % result just **one row** above a model with no translation ability at all). **The bar is ABSOLUTE — ≥ 85 % — and the split's OWN printed leniency floor travels beside the number, every time.** Printing "66.7 % → X %" as an improvement measure is a category error and is a QA FAIL.

#### 12b. ⚠️ THE FAILURE-SHAPE TRAP — self-grading, one level up

> **Nobody tunes against a burned failure list and then scores on a holdout containing those same shapes. That is the self-grading trap one level up, and it defeats a fresh seal completely.**

The five burned rows are **legitimate signal about a CLASS** — they are already spent, so reading them costs nothing. They are **never a licence to memorize a STRING.** Both halves of this are law:

- **THE TUNER'S DUTY: fix the CLASS, not the STRING.** An exemplar, alias or rule that only works on the exact burned sentence is a **QA FAIL**. Each taught class must be argued in the handoff: *what generalizes, and why.*
- **THE HOLDOUT AUTHOR'S DUTY: carry the same failure CLASSES, and NONE of the burned SURFACE FORMS.** A fresh holdout that repeats *"trebuchets"*, *"spend my gold"*, *"bowmen"*, *"the wizard"* or *"attack with the archers"* is testing memorization. **Different non-existent unit, different economy verb, different colloquial, different determiner, different dropped-selection phrasing** — and the handoff carries a table proving each new form is not a paraphrase of the burned one.
- **STRUCTURAL SEPARATION, NOT AN HONOUR SYSTEM.** The holdout task and the tuning task are **dispatched in the same wave, in the same message**, so *neither agent's output exists when the other starts.* The tuner may not read the fresh holdout; the holdout's author may not read the tuner's few-shots, synonym table, Zone A, or handoff.
- **QA IS THE ONLY ROLE THAT MAY OPEN A SEALED HOLDOUT, AND ONLY FOR THE DISJOINTNESS CHECK** (§11's literal-string rule, extended to near-paraphrase after the TASK-412 WARN-1 precedent). ⛔ **A QA report may not quote or paraphrase a single holdout sentence, Id or expected field** — QA reports are read by the tuner, and one quoted row leaks the seal. The `qa/TASK-416.md` §2c idiom is the model: **learn that a string is absent; learn nothing else.**
- **REACTIVE COVERAGE IS DECLARED** (unchanged from §11): if an alias or few-shot was added *because a corpus row needed it*, the handoff lists it.

⚠️ **AND THE GRAMMAR CANNOT CLOSE THIS CLASS — BY CONSTRUCTION, NOT BY OMISSION.** Both refusal failures were **structurally valid commands that were semantically wrong**. The grammar admits all 13 kinds and all 7 intents regardless of board state or policy (**§1: constrain identity hard, leave quantity soft** — deliberately), so an out-of-scope sentence always has a legal-looking production. **`{"ask":"unsupported"}` already exists in the grammar and the model simply did not route to it** — `HOLD-14` proves it can. ⇒ **The fix is few-shots and vocabulary. A task that responds to this by tightening the grammar is introducing the defect §1 names, not fixing one.**

#### 12c. ⚖️ THE ZONE-A GROWTH BUDGET IS IN TOKENS, AND `ContextTokens` STAYS FROZEN

Rungs 1 and 2 **grow Zone A** — more few-shots, a bigger synonym table. That is expected and is the point. It is also the one way this wave can silently break three bars that already PASSED.

- **CEILING FOR THIS WAVE: `zoneA_tok ≤ 1389`** (measured 1139 **+ 250**). Derivation, from measured operands: `1389 (Zone A) + 400 (MaxSnapshotTokens, the B+C cap) + 96 (MaxOutputTokens) + ~13 (chat-template wrapper) = 1898 of 2048`, leaving **~150 tok of margin**. **HARD GATE at the measurement task: over budget ⇒ STOP, do not score anything against an over-budget prompt.**
- ⛔ **`ContextTokens` STAYS 2048 THROUGH THIS WAVE.** Bars **#1, #3 and #4 already PASSED at this value**, and `n_ctx` drives KV-cache size and prefill cost — **raising it to buy Zone-A room would silently invalidate three passing measurements to solve a problem that is not yet biting.** Any request to raise it comes to Jonathan **with a re-measure of those three bars attached.** It is not a tuner's lever.
- **A CHAR-DERIVED TOKEN FIGURE IS `DERIVED`, NEVER `MEASURED`** (§8's instance-1 and instance-6 classes, both of which cost this batch a correction). The tuning agent has no model and **cannot measure tokens** — it states chars and the assumed ratio and labels the result DERIVED. **Only `Siege.Llama.SpikePrompt`'s printed counts are MEASURED.**
- **STATE THE TWO-SIDED EFFECT, DO NOT LEAVE IT IMPLIED.** Zone A is the **static prefix**: growing it **IMPROVES bar #3's reuse ratio** and **WORSENS turn-1 cold prefill**, which is bar #2's TTFT and the first sentence of every match. Both move; a handoff that names only the favourable one is not reporting.
- **ZONE B AND ZONE C BYTES DO NOT MOVE IN THIS WAVE.** The `t0` tripwire (`zoneB_chars = 68`, `zoneC_chars = 887`) must still hold at the measurement, or bar #3's reproduction is void and the corpus's premises have moved.

#### 12d. ⚖️ MANAGER RULING — BAR #2 IS A **WALL-CLOCK** BAR, AND ITS TOKEN COUNT IS RESTATED

Build-master correctly refused to resolve this in its own favour: the board's bar read *"TTFT **+** wall-clock for ~60 constrained tokens … ≤ 2 s partial · ≤ 6 s cpu"* — **one threshold, two quantities.** Bar text is the manager's, so I rule it, and I rule it in the conservative direction:

- **THE THRESHOLD GOVERNS WALL-CLOCK TO THE COMPLETE PARSED ORDER.** ⚠️ **TTFT is not player-visible for this feature and cannot be**: §3 says the model emits **symbols only** and every player-facing string is a game-authored template, so **there is no streaming surface** — nothing exists to show the player until the whole JSON has been emitted and parsed. A latency bar measured at a moment the player cannot observe is not a latency bar.
- **"~60 constrained tokens" IS RETIRED FROM THE BAR.** Real constrained output is **19–57 tokens (mean ~39)** — it stops at EOS after valid JSON. The bar is restated against **the real constrained output length**. ⚠️ **`wall60_equiv_ms` is an EXTRAPOLATION printed by the harness and MUST NEVER be quoted as a measurement.**
- ⇒ **UNDER THIS READING BAR #2 FAILS: partial 6472 ms vs ≤ 2000 · cpu 8730 ms vs ≤ 6000.** Full offload — not a named tier on the bar — measures **2106 ms with zero aborts.**
- ⛔ **AND THE UNAMBIGUOUS FACT OUTRANKS THE WORDING EITHER WAY: the CPU tier did not finish 2 of 5 generations and emitted truncated JSON. A truncated command is unusable, not slow.** No reading of the bar makes that a pass.
- ⚠️ **THE NUMBER THAT ACTUALLY MATTERS TO JONATHAN: the BEST tier is 2.1 s** — which is exactly the *"2-second pause mid-battle"* tolerance he named himself at TASK-415. **This ruling is flagged for his overturn at the next gate**: the bar's text is mine, the tolerance is his.
- **Decode is the whole cost** (ms/token: full ~36-49 · partial ~117-131 · cpu ~156-182; TTFT is nearly flat within a tier). 🚩 **RECORDED, NOT TASKED:** a more compact JSON schema would cut the full-offload wall roughly proportionally — that is **rung 3 ("tighter grammar") and is Jonathan's call**, not a tuning task's.

#### 12e. STANDING PROCEDURE ITEMS THAT TRAVEL WITH EVERY EDITOR TASK IN THIS LANE

- ⚠️ **THE GITIGNORED `Saved/Config/WindowsEditor/Engine.ini` PYTHON REMOTE-EXEC BLOCK IS DROPPED BY THE EDITOR'S SHUTDOWN REWRITE, EVERY TIME.** Confirmed across two sessions — **standing behaviour, not a one-off.** Any task needing that channel **re-appends it after every editor close.**
- ⚠️ **THE USER-GLOBAL `EditorSettings.ini` `bThrottleCPUWhenNotForeground=False` LINE IS LOAD-BEARING FOR EVERY BENCH.** A headless-driven PIE session is always background and idles at **exactly 3.0 FPS forever** with the throttle on — **a bar taken under it is meaningless, not conservative.** Verify measured FPS **≥ 58 live** immediately before benching; the run-twice rule is necessary and never sufficient.
- ⛔ **WARN-R2's LINE MUST NEVER BE QUOTED AS A LAYOUT FINDING.** `bound=BEST_CASE reused=0 DROP=0.9%` + *"THE PROMPT LAYOUT IS WRONG AND MUST BE FIXED BEFORE ANYTHING ELSE IS BUILT"* has fired **twice**, been checked twice, and been a **decode abort both times** — with the same run's `SHIPPED_WORST_CASE` bound healthy at 77.1 %. **A diagnostic that has misled every time it fired is a defect, and it is fixed, not merely warned about:** the layout verdict may only be emitted for a bound that **completed both turns**.

## Damage types (C++)
- UDamageType subclasses named `USiegeDamageType_<Kind>`, all declared in `Source/GitClaudeUnrealTest/Siegebound/DamageTypes.h/.cpp`
- Kinds: `Melee`, `Projectile`, `Siege` (**added M4, TASK-054**), `Spell` (**added M5, TASK-098**)
- Damage-vs-fortification scaling (GDD §3.0) reads `DamageEvent.DamageTypeClass`: Projectile = 50%, Melee/default = 100%, **`Siege` = 200%**. **M4 ruling:** the 200% applies to BOTH the castle (`ACastle::TakeDamage`) AND buildings (`ABuilding::TakeDamage`) — Siege units (Profile=Siege: Ogre, Sapper) tag their attacks with `USiegeDamageType_Siege`. Units and the hero always take the listed damage (no scaling). Attackers tag projectile/siege damage; melee needs no tag.
- **M5 ruling — `Spell` = 50% in `ACastle::TakeDamage` ONLY** (mirrors the M2 Projectile precedent, NOT the M4 Siege both-rule): buildings/units/hero take FULL listed spell damage — Lightning (200) must kill an Arrow Tower (150 HP), it is "the tower-killer" per GDD §4. All spell damage is applied with `USiegeDamageType_Spell`.

## Spells & Set III (M5)
Added 2026-07-08 (TASK-097..109; GDD §3.5/§3.11/§4 Set III/§7). Spells have NO actor blueprints — they resolve through code (see resolver). Crystal Tower is a normal building card (existing `BP_Building_<CardID>` / `SM_<CardID>` laws apply: `BP_Building_CrystalTower`, `SM_CrystalTower`; card art `T_CardArt_<CardID>` law applies to all 6 new CardIDs).
- **Set III CardIDs (row names, character-for-character):** `Fireball`, `FrostNova`, `Lightning`, `BattleCry`, `Pickpocket`, `CrystalTower`.
- **Spell resolver:** `USpellLibrary` (UBlueprintFunctionLibrary) in `Source/GitClaudeUnrealTest/Siegebound/SpellLibrary.h/.cpp` — the ONE home for spell resolution, shared by `ASiegePlayerController` (targeting mode) and `ASiegeBotController` (M5 bot rules). Entry point pinned: `static bool USpellLibrary::ResolveSpell(UWorld* World, FName CardID, const FCardRow& Row, ETeamId CasterTeam, const FVector& TargetPoint)`. Null-safe everywhere; no friendly fire (§3.0).
- **FCardRow M5 columns (typed one-per-concept, sparse; registry continues the M4 list):**
  - `SpellEffect` (`ESpellEffect` enum, `None`) — dispatch token: `None` / `AoEDamage` (Fireball) / `Freeze` (FrostNova) / `TopTargetsDamage` (Lightning) / `AllyBuff` (BattleCry) / `GoldSteal` (Pickpocket). Declared in CardRow.h. CSV cells use the enum value names.
  - `EffectDuration` (float, 0) — timed-effect seconds (FrostNova 4, BattleCry 8).
  - `MaxTargets` (int32, 0) — top-N target count (Lightning 3).
  - `GoldSteal` (int32, 0) — gold stolen, capped at what the victim has (Pickpocket 10).
  - `ChainTargets` (int32, 0) — Chain keyword arrives as its typed column per the M4 deferral note: total targets hit per attack (CrystalTower 3).
  - `ChainFalloff` (int32, 0) — flat damage reduction per bounce (CrystalTower 5 ⇒ 15/10/5 with Dmg 15).
  - Spells REUSE `Damage` (Fireball 100, Lightning 200) and `AoERadius` (Fireball 300, FrostNova 350, Lightning 400, BattleCry 400) — no duplicate radius/damage columns.
- **Mechanic rules (UPROPERTY defaults with GDD § comments, NOT CSV — M2/M4 precedent, e.g. Rally/Charge magnitudes):** BattleCry magnitudes (+50% attack speed, +25% move speed), `ChainBounceRadius` = 350 (bounce search radius from the previous target — GDD doesn't specify; manager-defined), freeze non-stacking refresh rule.
- **Targeting mode (§3.5/§7):** spells target ANYWHERE on the map (no half restriction); the ground reticle is TRACE-projected onto whatever surface is under the cursor (terrain-ready for M4.5 hills — LAW, not a follow-up); LMB confirms (gold deducted at confirm — card-leaves-hand-at-CONFIRM law), RMB/Esc cancels free. **Exception (manager ruling):** `GoldSteal` spells resolve INSTANTLY on play (no reticle for a global effect — recorded deviation from §3.5; CardType stays Spell).
- **Spell VFX (code contract):** every spell resolve spawns `/Game/VFX/NS_Spell_<CardID>` (Niagara, Content/VFX/), soft path COMPOSED from the CardID (mirrors the BP soft-class-path law), null-safe: missing system ⇒ log once, resolve anyway. Crystal Tower's chain visual is `/Game/VFX/NS_ChainZap`. Donor-duplicate authoring from Variant_Combat Niagara systems is acceptable at M5 (template-donor rule); §6 one-frame readability bar.
- **Targeting reticle material:** `M_SpellReticle` (Content/Materials/, DeferredDecal domain — M_CenterlineStripe recipe), soft-referenced by the targeting-mode code, null-safe (missing ⇒ no visual, targeting still works, log once).
- **Spell VFX element re-skin (M7, TASK-187):** the fire/ice spell systems are RE-SKINNED to the imported `Content/Fire_Magic/` + `Content/Ice_Magic/` Niagara packs AT THEIR EXISTING code-contract paths — `/Game/VFX/NS_Spell_Fireball` (← `Fire_Magic/VFX_Niagara/NS_Fire_Magic_Explosion` or `_AOE`) and `/Game/VFX/NS_Spell_FrostNova` (← `Ice_Magic/VFX_Niagara/NS_Ice_Magic_Shockwave`/`_Frozen`/`_Snowstorm`). **NO rename, NO new data column:** `USpellLibrary::ResolveSpell` COMPOSES the path from the CardID (SpellLibrary.cpp:77-78), so the re-skin MUST land at these exact placeholder paths (a data column cannot point at a `Fire_Magic` asset). This is a VFX LOOK-SWAP, NOT a new card — Fireball (cards.csv row 24, `DeckCount 2`, in deck) + FrostNova (row 25, `DeckCount 0`, NOT in the default deck — see flag) already exist from M5.

## Spell delivery overhaul (2026-07-21, Jonathan playtest directive — amends "Spells & Set III (M5)" delivery law)
- **Fireball + FrostNova become HERO-ORIGIN LINE spells:** cast fires FROM the hero a short distance forward — the hitbox is a LINE IN THE AIR (projectile travel or capsule sweep, programmer's call at TASK-236), replacing the reticle-placed ground-circle AoE. Aim direction = toward the cursor/reticle point (**CONFIRMED by Jonathan 2026-07-21 — "cursor aim is fine"; no longer provisional**). Targeting mode is KEPT (reticle sets the aim direction; LMB confirm fires; deduct-at-confirm + refund laws unchanged). Damage/cost/castle-50%/friendly-fire laws UNCHANGED initially — line-vs-circle coverage is a PLAYTEST WATCH, not a balance change.
- **Bot cast origin for line spells (flagged design default):** the bot has no hero — its line spells originate from its own castle toward its chosen target point (player = hero origin). Flagged for QA + playtest; the `ResolveSpell` pinned signature stays (TargetPoint semantics shift from impact-center to AIM-POINT for the two line spells).
- **Lightning stays a sky strike** — bigger + taller: AoERadius is a cards.csv DATA change (400 → 700, flagged playtest number) and the VISUAL circle + HITBOX must scale together honestly from the data (no visual-only lying); the strike reads from higher in the sky.
- **VFX contract unchanged in name:** effects stay IN PLACE at `/Game/VFX/NS_Spell_<CardID>` (composed-path law). Donors: `Content/Fire_Magic/` + `Content/Ice_Magic/` (projectile trail + impact looks), `Content/sA_ArcheryVfxPack/` lightning assets (reuse-ledger law; donors READ-ONLY).
- **`SpellDelivery` data column (registered 2026-07-22, QA WARN-2 closure — TASK-248):** FCardRow column declaring the per-spell delivery mode; the value strings and parsing are PINNED BY CODE in SpellLibrary.h (TASK-236 lineage — line-delivery for Fireball/FrostNova, sky-strike for Lightning); unset/illegal cell ⇒ `Auto` (per-effect inference: AoEDamage/Freeze → HeroLine, all others → legacy ground circle; never a crash — corrected 2026-07-22 per qa/TASK-248-qa.md WARN: the earlier "always legacy ground-circle" wording was a wrong promise for future unset cards). Lives in cards.csv per the §3.0 single-source law; TASK-248 appends the header cell + records the exact value set in its handoff.

The §6 audio hooks (TASK-179, C++, null-safe) play a sound at each gameplay event via the COMPOSED soft path `/Game/Audio/S_<Event>` — a code contract (like the `SM_<CardID>` / `NS_Spell_<CardID>` string laws), null-safe (missing ⇒ silent + log once). - **Loop/2D flags (match the TASK-179 code contract):** `S_MinerClink` LOOPS (started on mining/arrival, stopped on death); `S_VictoryMusic`/`S_DefeatMusic`/`S_OvertimeSting`/`S_CardPlay`/`S_CardDiscard` are 2D (`PlaySound2D`); the rest are world SFX (`SpawnSoundAtLocation`).
- **Raw sources** checked in under `Content/RawAssets/Audio/` (raw-asset rule) when the source is a loose file rather than an imported pack.

## Material & Niagara lane laws
- **Custom-HLSL BAN (codified 2026-07-22 from TASK-245; previously lived only in orchestrator memory + the TASK-239 handoff):** NEVER use a Custom HLSL node in materials on this project — under the Substrate + HW-RT stack it detonates shader permutations and wedges the editor (TASK-239: 0-for-2, ~4 h lost; the stock-node rebuild compiles in seconds — TASK-245 proof). STOCK NODES + PARAMETERS ONLY; parameter iteration never recompiles. Evidence: handoffs/TASK-239.md.
- **UE 5.8 Niagara/material lane notes (pointer):** three engine findings from the TASK-245 session — the sprite-VF TexCoord bug, per-instance ObjectPositionWS behavior, and the dead attributeBindings lane — are documented in `handoffs/TASK-239.md` (FINAL SESSION). Read before authoring sprite-based Niagara or per-instance material logic.
- **Instanced-mesh material flag law (codified 2026-07-22, TASK-249):** any material consumed via `FScatterLayer.OverrideMaterial` (or ANY runtime HISM `SetMaterial`) MUST set `bUsedWithInstancedStaticMeshes=true` — without it the HISM silently renders the gray WorldGridMaterial default. TASK-249 evidence: the first wired seed rendered gray with `OverrideMaterials[0]` correctly set; the flag fixed it. Evidence: handoffs/TASK-249.md.

## Raw asset sources
- Blender FBX exports live in `Content/RawAssets/<AssetNameWithoutPrefix>.fbx` (e.g., `Castle.fbx` → `SM_Castle`); the FBX is checked into Git alongside the imported .uasset

## Textured mesh law (TRELLIS.2 art pipeline)
Added 2026-07-07 (TASK-082..088, Jonathan-approved plan: automated TRELLIS.2 → Blender → UE5 pipeline). Governs game-ready textured meshes produced by `Tools/ArtPipeline/` (pilot scope: Footman, Archer, Castle; the remaining M7 blockouts follow this template). `<AssetName>` = CardID for card actors, `Castle` for the castle.
- **Active pipeline assets:** Footman · Archer · Castle (pilot, TASK-082..088) + **Ogre** — pulled forward from the M7 batch by Jonathan's 2026-07-14 directive (TASK-147..152). NO new naming pattern: the Ogre follows this law verbatim — `SM_Ogre` (same-path overwrite of the blockout) / `T_Ogre_D|_N|_ORM` / `MI_Ogre_PBR` from `M_AssetPBR` / slots [`TeamRegion`, `OgrePBR`], UNIT path (feet-center, ≤15k tris, 1024² bakes, ≤4 generated hulls, `ucx:null`). Concept casing note: the drop arrived lowercase `ogre.png`; the AssetName is PascalCase `Ogre` (reconciled at TASK-147). The remaining 15 blockouts stay deferred to M7.
- **Concepts:** the ACCEPTED concept image for each produced asset is committed at `Content/RawAssets/Concepts/<AssetName>.png`. Working inputs live in `Tools/ArtPipeline/Inbox/<AssetName>.png` and intermediates in `Tools/ArtPipeline/Cache/<AssetName>/` — both gitignored.
- **Stage 0 — concept generation (M7, Jonathan-AUTHORIZED 2026-07-16; TASK-184 build / TASK-185 run):** concepts may be AUTO-GENERATED by `Tools/ArtPipeline/concept_generate.py` — a text→image client that mirrors `trellis_generate.py`'s structure, HF_TOKEN ENV-ONLY secret-handling (guard-secrets `hf_` law), and CLI/exit-code discipline (drives an HF text-to-image Space/model via the existing HF PRO token). Per-CardID art-direction prompts live in `Tools/ArtPipeline/concept_prompts.json` (art-director-authored). Output = `Tools/ArtPipeline/Inbox/<CardID>.png` (PascalCase AssetName = CardID — the existing Inbox law; the tool writes PascalCase directly, so no casing reconcile is needed). Jonathan may review/REPLACE any generated concept before its TRELLIS Stage-1 wave consumes it (OPTIONAL / NON-blocking — TASK-167). This REPLACES the hand-drop concept gate for the 16 M7 blockouts. `concept_generate.py` is `Tools/**/*.py` = CODE → the tooling QA gate applies before commit.
- **✍️ FLUX.1-dev PROMPT LAW (added 2026-08-01; learned the expensive way across 8 rolls at TASK-369 — binds every entry in `concept_prompts.json`).** Two rules, both measured on real rolls, not style advice:
  - **(1) WRITE IN POSITIVE FORM ONLY — IN-PROMPT NEGATIONS *SUMMON* THEIR TOKENS.** `negative_prompt` is **dropped entirely** by FLUX.1-dev (it is guidance-distilled, so there is no CFG channel to carry it), which means every defence has to live in the positive prompt — and negating there **backfires**. Demonstrated on roll 2: *"not rounded pauldrons"* → **got rounded pauldrons**; *"absolutely NO branching antler rack"* → **got a branching antler rack**. Rolls 3+ were rewritten with **zero** negations and immediately behaved. **Never write "no X" / "not X" / "without X" — describe the thing you DO want instead** ("flat slab mantle" beats "no rounded pauldrons"). The `negative_prompt` field stays authored in `concept_prompts.json` for a future CFG-model swap; **it is inert today and must never be relied on.**
  - **(2) HARD STYLE CLIFF AT ≈1.6k CHARACTERS — KEEP ENTRIES ≲1,600.** The shared style block (*"Warcraft Rumble tier, 2.5–3 heads tall, hand-painted"*) sits at the **END** of every entry, so a long prompt dilutes exactly the tokens that keep the roster coherent. At **2,007 chars** (roll 3) the tail washed out and the roll lost the roster style, the chunky proportions **and** two identity props at once. Back at **~1,570** (rolls 4–6) the style returned. The other **18 shipped concepts are 700–900 chars**, which is why this never surfaced before — the cliff is only reachable on an unusually specified subject. If an identity genuinely needs more than ~1,600 chars, **cut description, not the style tail** (or move the style block to the front and re-validate).
  - **Reproducibility:** a shipped concept entry pins its winning **seed** so `concept_generate.py <Asset> --force` reproduces it byte-for-byte (Sorcerer = seed 71022, 1,573 chars). Record the roll count and both gate verdicts in the handoff.
- **Mesh swap:** the refined FBX exports to the EXISTING blockout path `Content/RawAssets/<AssetName>.fbx` and imports OVERWRITING `/Game/Meshes/SM_<AssetName>` in place — same-path swaps keep every code/BP soft reference (including the placement-ghost `/Game/Meshes/SM_<CardID>` string contract) intact. NEVER delete+recreate the SM asset.
- **Textures:** `/Game/Textures/T_<AssetName>_D` (sRGB) · `T_<AssetName>_N` (normal) · `T_<AssetName>_ORM` (packed Occlusion/Roughness/Metallic, LINEAR — sRGB off). PNG sources checked in at `Content/RawAssets/Textures/<AssetName>/`. Bake sizes: 1024² units / 2048² buildings.
- **Two-slot material contract (requires ZERO C++ changes):** every pipeline mesh has EXACTLY two material slots, in order — **slot 0 named `TeamRegion`** (a minority face-set: trim/banners/accents; the BeginPlay team recolor hardcodes `MI_TeamColor_<Team>` onto slot 0 — SummonedUnit.cpp:146 / Building.cpp:80 / Castle.cpp:125 — and keeps working unchanged; author `MI_TeamColor_Blue` as the design-time placeholder), **slot 1 named `<AssetName>PBR`** assigned `MI_<AssetName>_PBR` (Content/Materials/Instances/), an instance of the master **`/Game/Materials/M_AssetPBR`** whose texture parameters are named exactly `BaseColor`, `Normal`, `ORM`. The placement ghost tints ALL slots (already true today).
- **Mesh settings:** **Nanite OFF** on pipeline meshes (ruling). UV layer named exactly `UVMap`. Tri budgets: units ≤15k, castle ≤40k (per `pipeline_manifest.json`). Origins: units feet-center, buildings ground-center. Export axis contract: `axis_forward='-Z'`, `axis_up='Y'`, `apply_unit_scale`, FACE smoothing. Collision: units ≤4 simple hulls; buildings author explicit `UCX_SM_<AssetName>` geometry — the castle UCX must be wall-footprint-exact (M1 plinth ~410-unit dead-zone lesson) with bounds within ±10% of the blockout.
- **Tooling law:** `Tools/**/*.py` is CODE — the full QA gate applies before commit. **`HF_TOKEN` is ENV-ONLY**: read from the environment at runtime; never written to any file, never passed on argv, never echoed/logged (guard-secrets hook carries the `hf_` pattern). Heavy Blender refine runs HEADLESS via `blender.exe --background --python` through Bash — the live Blender MCP bridge has a 30 s socket cap and is for <30 s inspection/preview only.
- **Fab quarantine:** marketplace packs land in `Content/Fab/<Pack>/` and are READ-ONLY donors (the Variant_* rule applies: soft-reference or duplicate-into-/Game/, never edit in place). Acquisition is HUMAN-ONLY via `.claude/pipeline/fab/FAB-REQUESTS.md` (FAB-### entries: requested → approved → fulfilled → integrated; Jonathan fulfills via the Epic Launcher). Agents never browse/buy/download Fab.
- **Lane isolation:** this pipeline NEVER writes `Content/RawAssets/CardArt/` or `/Game/UI/CardArt/` — those belong to the card-art chain (TASK-077..081, "Card artwork (hand UI)" law).
- **M7 batch scope (16 remaining blockouts, TASK-166..173):** the M7 art pass upgrades ALL remaining un-textured blockouts through this law verbatim. UNIT path (feet-center, ≤15k tris, 1024² bakes, two-slot `[TeamRegion, <CardID>PBR]`, ≤4 hulls, `ucx:null`): `Knight`, `Miner`, `Cavalry`, `Cleric`, `Longbowman`, `MilitiaMob`, `Pikeman`, `Sapper`. BUILDING path (ground-center, 2048² bakes, authored `UCX_SM_<CardID>` wall-footprint-exact hulls, two-slot `[TeamRegion, <CardID>PBR]`): `ArrowTower`, `Wall`, `BombTower`, `BallistaTower`, `Barracks`, `DeepMine`, `CrystalTower`. Each same-path overwrites `/Game/Meshes/SM_<CardID>` (soft-ref parity, never delete+recreate). Concepts arrive at `Tools/ArtPipeline/Inbox/<CardID>.png` (PascalCase AssetName = CardID; casing reconciled at TASK-166) — the source-of-concepts gate (TASK-167) precedes all Stage-1 generation. Footman/Archer/Castle/Ogre are already shipped and are NOT re-run.
- **GoldNode emissive economy-prop VARIANT (M7 ruling, TASK-166/171):** `SM_GoldNode` IS in the M7 batch (all 16 upgraded) but is the ONE exception to the two-slot TeamRegion contract — it is the §"Team contract" team-color EXCEPTION (gold nodes carry emissive glow regardless of team; `AGoldNode` never runs the BeginPlay slot-0 recolor and is a level prop, not a card actor). It therefore takes a SINGLE-slot PBR+EMISSIVE material with NO `TeamRegion` slot: slot 0 named `GoldNodePBR` assigned `MI_GoldNode_PBR` (from `M_AssetPBR`) with the warm-yellow emissive PRESERVED — either keep the existing authored `M_GoldGlow` emissive on a dedicated slot OR bake a `T_GoldNode_E` emissive channel wired into `MI_GoldNode_PBR` (art-director's call at TASK-171; §6 "gold glows warm yellow (emissive)" is the bar). Manifest `team_region:null`, BUILDING path (2048²/ground-center). Everything else follows the law (same-path overwrite of `/Game/Meshes/SM_GoldNode`, Nanite off, `UVMap`). Concept `Inbox/GoldNode.png`. **⚠️ AS-SHIPPED CORRECTION (2026-07-27, TASK-309 audit): the `MI_GoldNode_PBR` option was NOT what shipped — `SM_GoldNode` slot 0 carries `M_GoldGlow` directly and `T_GoldNode_*` are ORPHANED (referenced by nothing on screen). Same for CrystalTower / `MI_CrystalGlow` / `T_CrystalTower_*`. See "GoldNode / CrystalTower glow materials" below before touching either — a texture re-bake for these two changes NOTHING.**

## Meshy second engine — Stage-1.5 retexture & image-to-3D alternative (M7.5)
Added 2026-07-18 (TASK-191; Jonathan-approved Art Quality Upgrade directive, plan `C:\Users\wesel\.claude\plans\i-am-a-bit-sunny-bird.md`). Meshy Pro is a SECOND ENGINE inside the existing TRELLIS.2 pipeline — it changes only the mesh/texture SOURCE. It NEVER replaces the Stage-2/Stage-3 laws above.
- **Secret law:** the canonical env var is **`MESHY_TOKEN`** (AMENDED 2026-07-18 — TASK-197 DONE: Jonathan created the Meshy premium account and set the key as user env var `MESHY_TOKEN`, not the originally-specced `MESHY_API_KEY`; his actual setup is law. `MESHY_API_KEY` survives only as a DEPRECATED alias — tools read `MESHY_TOKEN` and must never require the alias). ENV-ONLY — exactly the `HF_TOKEN` discipline: read from the environment at runtime, never written to any file, never passed on argv, never echoed/logged. TASK-198 adds the observed Meshy key prefix to the guard-secrets hook once seen. HKCU User scope — note the Windows-rollback-wipes-HKCU precedent (uv/HF machine-config memory). Norton: NO exclusion pre-added — run BARE first (the proven playbook); TASK-198's live run adjudicates, and only a surfaced-VERBATIM TLS/cert failure justifies adding `meshy.ai` / `*.meshy.ai` exclusions. Never disable verification.
- **Tool:** `Tools/ArtPipeline/meshy_generate.py` (TASK-198) mirrors `trellis_generate.py` in structure, CLI, secret-handling, and exit-code discipline (0 ok/`--check` · 2 key unset · 3 quota/credits exhausted, expected pause · 4 API drift · 5 input missing · 64 usage). Two modes: `--mode retexture` (Stage 1.5) and `--mode image3d` (Stage-1 alternative). `Tools/**/*.py` is CODE → the tooling QA gate applies.
- **Stage 1.5 — RETEXTURE (the dark-albedo fix):** input = the DENSE DONOR `Cache/<CardID>/trellis_raw.glb` (or `meshy_raw.glb`) + the per-CardID concept `Inbox/<CardID>.png` as style reference; output `Cache/<CardID>/meshy_retex.glb`. Stage 2 then consumes the retextured donor UNCHANGED (cleanup → conform → remesh/decimate → UV → bake D/N/ORM → two-slot TeamRegion split → UCX → FBX) — only the texture source improves.
- **Stage 1 ALTERNATIVE — IMAGE-TO-3D:** the same concept PNG in → `Cache/<CardID>/meshy_raw.glb` out. Stage 2 consumes whichever raw GLB the asset's state/manifest selects. TRELLIS.2 stays the DEFAULT engine (speed + free quota); Meshy is per-asset/per-class per the A/B ruling (hero assets are the expected Meshy/1536³ candidates).
- **Provenance:** `Cache/<CardID>/state.json` records `engine` (`trellis` | `meshy-retex` | `meshy-i23d`), the Meshy task id(s), and the input-GLB sha; `refine_report.json` carries the `engine` field forward. `pipeline_manifest.json` MAY pin a per-asset `engine` preference (absent = `trellis`) and an optional per-asset `albedo_delight` override (the Stage-2 de-light step, TASK-193, defaults conservative-ON in-script).
- **INVARIANT (load-bearing):** Stage-2 + Stage-3 laws are UNTOUCHED regardless of engine — two-slot `[TeamRegion, <CardID>PBR]` (GoldNode emissive variant excepted), tri budgets, `UVMap`, Nanite OFF, same-path `SM_<CardID>` reimport (never delete+recreate), UCX rules. Meshy output NEVER lands directly in Content/ — everything re-enters through Stage 2.
- **A/B fleet gate (law):** NO fleet Meshy rollout (retexture or image-to-3D) before a Jonathan-approved A/B on a recorded-dark asset (Ogre; Knight optional second sample), judged from the Stage-2 refine_report preview renders. One asset proves the lane, then the fleet — the Ogre/rig-spike precedent. **GATE SATISFIED 2026-07-21 (TASK-200 ruled in substance by Jonathan's playtest feedback — see the board).**
- **Color-fidelity acceptance bar (added 2026-07-21, Jonathan playtest directive):** every retexture/image-to-3D output is accepted per-asset against its concept — the baked result must READ AS THE CONCEPT PNG'S PALETTE (`Content/RawAssets/Concepts/<CardID>.png`), not merely brighter. Meshy runs use `Inbox/<CardID>.png` as the style reference + the delight defaults; the eyeball comparison vs the concept is part of each wave's acceptance.
- **Animation (auto-rig + preset clips):** Meshy auto-rig FBX + preset clip library (idle/walk/attack/death — clips are credit-free) enter via the UE5 IK Retargeter: IK Rig assets `IK_<SkeletonName>` + retargeter `RTG_<Source>_to_<Target>` in Content/Characters/ (prefix table). Retargeted output clips OVERWRITE the existing `A_<CardID>_<Action>` sequences AT THEIR EXISTING PATHS (`/Game/Characters/Anims/`) — the same-path law preserves the TASK-189 composed-soft-path triggers and all ABP wiring; `SK_<CardID>`/`SKEL_SiegeBiped` contracts unchanged. Raw Meshy rigged/clip FBX checked in under `Content/RawAssets/Characters/Meshy/<CardID>/`. Pre-approval spike output goes to SCRATCH paths (never overwrite live clips before the eyeball gate).
- **UE-5.8 retarget-export defect + SANCTIONED export path (added 2026-07-19, TASK-229 ruling):** ALL THREE IK-Retargeter export doors (commandlet, `IKRetargetBatchOperation` editor-python API, and the UI "Export Selected Animations" button) route into the same `FIKRetargetBatchOperation`, which exports ROOT-ONLY motion in UE 5.8 (preview is correct; evidence handoffs/TASK-221-222.md §7 — Jonathan-eyewitnessed). The SANCTIONED export path for Meshy clips is therefore the BLENDER-SIDE retarget tool `Tools/ArtPipeline/retarget_meshy_to_siegebiped.py` (maps the Meshy 24-bone Mixamo-style rig onto the 21-bone SiegeBiped via the IK_MeshyBiped 9-chain map incl. the spine-reversal quirk; exports per-anim FBX for DIRECT import onto the existing SK skeleton). The `IK_`/`RTG_` assets remain for in-editor PREVIEW/verification only — never as the export route. Amplitude acceptance floor (HEIGHT-NORMALIZED, amended 2026-07-19 TASK-232 ruling): a retargeted Walk must show foot amplitude ≥ **40 uu × (unit height / 1.75 m)** — 40 uu at the standard-biped reference height, scaling linearly for short/tall units (MilitiaMob @ 1.49 m ⇒ floor ≈34 uu) — verified BOTH in Blender AND after UE import. The ~7 uu root-only signature is an ABSOLUTE failure regardless of height. Borderline passes still take a PIE visual verdict (conservative-rollout law).

## Skeletal rig & animation workstream (M7)
Added 2026-07-15 (TASK-153; Jonathan M7 directive — replace the blockout-tier procedural-lunge attack (GDD §3.8) with REAL skeletal-mesh animation). There is NO rig/anim tooling in the project today; it is stood up FROM SCRATCH and PROVEN on ONE character (the rig spike, TASK-160/161 — Jonathan-gated) BEFORE any batch rigging (TASK-163/164), exactly as the Ogre proved the static TRELLIS.2 pipeline before its batch. The HERO is ALREADY skeletal (template Manny + `ABP_Manny_Combat`/`AM_ComboAttack` donors) and is UNCHANGED by this workstream — it covers the SUMMONED UNITS only (buildings do not animate; tower recoil stays the procedural static-mesh motion, TASK-155).
- **Skeletal mesh:** `SK_<CardID>` in Content/Characters/ (`/Game/Characters/SK_<CardID>`), e.g. `SK_Footman`. Rigged FROM the game-ready textured mesh (`SM_<CardID>` from the TRELLIS.2 batch) so the animated runtime mesh carries the SAME materials. Raw rigged FBX checked in at `Content/RawAssets/Characters/<CardID>.fbx` (raw-asset rule). The SK mesh KEEPS the same two-slot material contract as its SM source — slots ordered [0] `TeamRegion` → `MI_TeamColor_<Team>` (BeginPlay recolor drives slot 0, unchanged), [1] `<CardID>PBR` → `MI_<CardID>_PBR` — so team recolor works identically on the skeletal path. Nanite OFF; ≤15k tris (unit budget); feet-center origin; `UVMap`.
- **Skeleton:** PREFER a SHARED humanoid skeleton `SKEL_SiegeBiped` (`/Game/Characters/SKEL_SiegeBiped`) that humanoid units retarget onto (author locomotion once, share across units); a non-humanoid / oddly-proportioned unit MAY carry its own auto-created `SK_<CardID>_Skeleton` **only on an explicit manager ruling** — it is a NON-DEFAULT, never an assumption. The chosen skeleton per asset is RECORDED in the task handoff. **AS SHIPPED (corrected 2026-07-26): the planned `SKEL_SiegeBiped` name was never used — the shipped shared skeleton is `SK_Footman_Skeleton` (`/Game/Characters/SK_Footman_Skeleton`, auto-created from the Footman rig) and it is the SOLE skeleton asset in `/Game/Characters`. NO bespoke per-unit skeleton exists. In particular `SK_Ogre_Skeleton` DOES NOT EXIST AND NEVER DID — the Ogre (the unit this bullet once cited as the example) binds the shared `SK_Footman_Skeleton`; see the "Fleet Meshy remaster" shared-skeleton binding law below for the full evidence chain and the root cause of the myth.**
